```
# Tests for brepper

# Use Catch2 for testing
include(FetchContent)
FetchContent_Declare(
    Catch2
    GIT_REPOSITORY https://github.com/catchorg/Catch2.git
    GIT_TAG v3.5.2
)
FetchContent_MakeAvailable(Catch2)

# Include Catch2 CMake extras for test discovery
list(APPEND CMAKE_MODULE_PATH ${catch2_SOURCE_DIR}/extras)
include(Catch)

# Test data directory
set(TEST_DATA_DIR "${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}")
configure_file(
    "${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}/test_config.hpp.in"
    "${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}/test_config.hpp"
)

# Collect all test source files
set(TEST_SOURCES
    test_stl_reader.cpp
    test_mesh_sampling.cpp
    test_onshape_models.cpp
    test_e2e.cpp
    test_step_comparison.cpp
)

# Create test executable
add_executable(brepper_tests ${TEST_SOURCES})

target_include_directories(brepper_tests PRIVATE
    ${PCL_INCLUDE_DIRS}
    ${OpenCASCADE_INCLUDE_DIR}
    ${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/src
    ${CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR}
)

target_link_directories(brepper_tests PRIVATE
    ${PCL_LIBRARY_DIRS}
)

# OpenCASCADE libraries needed (same as main executable)
set(OCCT_LIBS
    TKernel TKMath TKG3d TKGeomBase TKBRep
    TKGeomAlgo TKTopAlgo TKShHealing TKFillet
    TKXSBase TKDESTEP
)

# Link against brepper library sources (exclude main.cpp)
file(GLOB_RECURSE LIB_SOURCES ${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/src/*.cpp)
list(FILTER LIB_SOURCES EXCLUDE REGEX "main\\.cpp$")

target_sources(brepper_tests PRIVATE ${LIB_SOURCES})

target_link_libraries(brepper_tests PRIVATE
    ${PCL_LIBRARIES}
    ${OCCT_LIBS}
    Eigen3::Eigen
    Catch2::Catch2WithMain
    $<$<BOOL:${OpenMP_CXX_FOUND}>:OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX>
    fmt::fmt
)

target_compile_definitions(brepper_tests PRIVATE
    ${PCL_DEFINITIONS}
    $<$<BOOL:${OpenMP_CXX_FOUND}>:BREPPER_USE_OPENMP>
)

# Register tests with CTest
catch_discover_tests(brepper_tests)

```

````
# Brepper - STL to STEP Converter

Convert triangulated STL meshes from CAD exports into parametric STEP files with fitted analytic and freeform surfaces.

## Usage

```
brepper - Convert STL mesh to STEP with fitted surfaces

USAGE:
    brepper [OPTIONS] <input.stl> [-o <output.step>]

REQUIRED:
    <input.stl>              Input STL file (binary or ASCII)
    -o, --output <file>      Output STEP file (required unless --stage < 6)

GENERAL OPTIONS:
    -v, --verbose            Enable verbose output
    -q, --quiet              Suppress non-error output
    --debug                  Enable debug output and intermediate files
    --threads <N>            Number of threads (default: auto)
    --stage <1-6>            Stop after stage (default: 6=export)
                             1=load, 2=segment, 3=assign, 4=boundary, 5=brep, 6=export
    --dimensions             Print mesh bounding box dimensions
```

### Examples

```bash
# Full conversion
./build/brepper input.stl -o output.step

# Just load and sample mesh (stage 1)
./build/brepper input.stl --stage 1 -v

# Run through segmentation (stage 2)
./build/brepper input.stl --stage 2 -v
```

See [DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md](DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md) for full documentation.

### Generating Test Models

Some tests use models generated via [CodeCAD](https://codecad.xyz/). The generated STL/STEP files are committed to the repo, so CodeCAD is not required to run tests. 

To add or modify these models:
1. Install CodeCAD
2. Edit files in `tests/ccad/parts/`
3. Run `./tests/ccad/generate_models.sh`

## License

MIT License
````

## Crate Structure

### `occt-sys` (v0.6.0)
- **Role**: Builds the C++ OCCT static libraries from the git submodule using cmake
- **Cargo.toml**: Located at [crates/occt-sys/Cargo.toml](/Users/sethml/src/opencascade-rs/crates/occt-sys/Cargo.toml)
- **Dependency**: `cmake = "0.1"`
- No Rust API — only a build script that produces `libTK*.a` static libs

### `opencascade-sys` (v0.2.0)
- **Role**: Auto-generated Rust FFI bindings — 8,700+ types, 164,000+ `extern "C"` functions across 324 modules
- **Cargo.toml**: Located at [crates/opencascade-sys/Cargo.toml](/Users/sethml/src/opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys/Cargo.toml)
- **Features**: `builtin` (default) — enables `occt-sys` dep to compile OCCT from source. Without it, links to system OCCT.
- **All generated code** lives in `crates/opencascade-sys/generated/` (ffi.rs, wrappers.cpp, and per-module `.rs` files)

### `opencascade` (v0.2.0)
- **Role**: High-level Rust wrapper with nice types (`Shape`, `Face`, `Edge`, `Wire`, `Solid`)
- **Cargo.toml**: Located at [crates/opencascade/Cargo.toml](/Users/sethml/src/opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade/Cargo.toml)
- **Deps**: `opencascade-sys`, `glam` (for DVec3), `kicad-parser`, `thiserror`
- Has `read_step()`, `write_step()`, and `ProjectPointOnSurf` usage already

### `opencascade-binding-generator`
- ~16k lines of Rust; parses OCCT headers with libclang
- README at [crates/opencascade-binding-generator/README.md](/Users/sethml/src/opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-binding-generator/README.md) — comprehensive documentation of the generated API patterns

### How to depend (path dependencies)
```toml
# Just the FFI layer:
[dependencies]
opencascade-sys = { path = "../opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys" }

# Or the high-level crate (which re-exports opencascade-sys):
[dependencies]
opencascade = { path = "../opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade" }

# To disable building OCCT from source and link to system OCCT:
opencascade-sys = { path = "...", default-features = false }
```

---

## PORTING.md (Full contents above)

Key patterns from [crates/opencascade-sys/PORTING.md](/Users/sethml/src/opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys/PORTING.md):

- **Module naming**: `BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge` → `b_rep_builder_api::MakeEdge`
- **Constructors**: `::new_<type_suffix>()` with compressed parameter types
- **Ownership**: All objects returned as `OwnedPtr<T>` (auto-deref via `Deref`/`DerefMut`)
- **Handles**: `Type::to_handle(obj)` consumes `OwnedPtr<T>` → `OwnedPtr<HandleT>`, `.get()` to deref
- **Handle upcasting**: `.to_handle_surface()`, `.to_handle_curve()` etc.
- **Handle downcasting**: `.downcast_to_plane()` returns `Option<OwnedPtr<HandleDerived>>`
- **Enums**: Typed Rust enums directly (not raw i32), e.g. `top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face`
- **Shape downcasting**: `unsafe { &*topo_ds::face(shape_ref) }` — requires unsafe
- **Static methods → free functions**: Utility classes become module-level functions

---

## API Available for Your Use Case

### 1. Reading STL Files

**Module `stl_api`** — `StlAPI_Reader`:
```rust
use opencascade_sys::stl_api;
use opencascade_sys::topo_ds;

let mut reader = stl_api::Reader::new();
let mut shape = topo_ds::Shape::new();
let ok = reader.read(&mut shape, "model.stl");
```

**Module `rw_stl`** — lower-level free functions returning `HandlePolyTriangulation`:
```rust
use opencascade_sys::rw_stl;
use opencascade_sys::message;

let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
// Returns HandlePolyTriangulation directly (no shape topology needed)
let tri_handle = rw_stl::read_file_charptr_progressrange_2("model.stl", &progress);
```

### 2. Reading STEP Files

**Module `step_control`** — `STEPControl_Reader`:
```rust
use opencascade_sys::{step_control, message, if_select};

let mut reader = step_control::Reader::new();
let status = reader.read_file_charptr("model.step");
// status should be if_select::ReturnStatus::Retvoid for success

let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
reader.transfer_roots(&progress);  // or transfer_root(1, &progress) for one root
let shape = reader.one_shape();    // → OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Shape>
```

The high-level crate has a convenience wrapper at [crates/opencascade/src/primitives/shape.rs](shape.rs#L491):
```rust
let shape = Shape::read_step("model.step")?;
```

### 3. Exploring Topology (TopExp_Explorer)

**Module `top_exp`** — iterating faces:
```rust
use opencascade_sys::{top_exp, top_abs, topo_ds};

let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
    shape.as_ref(),  // &topo_ds::Shape
    top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
    top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
);

while explorer.more() {
    let face_shape = explorer.value();        // &topo_ds::Shape
    let face = unsafe { &*topo_ds::face(face_shape) };  // &topo_ds::Face
    // ... work with face
    explorer.next();
}
```

Also available: `top_exp::map_shapes()` to populate an `IndexedMapOfShape` for deduplicated access.

### 4. Computing Distance from a Point to a Surface

**Option A: `GeomAPI_ProjectPointOnSurf`** — project a point onto a geometric surface:
```rust
use opencascade_sys::{b_rep, geom_api, extrema, gp};

// Extract the geometric surface from a face
let surface_handle = b_rep::Tool::surface_face(&face);

// Create projector (can reuse for multiple points)
let point = gp::Pnt::new_real3(x, y, z);
let mut projector = geom_api::ProjectPointOnSurf::new_pnt_handlegeomsurface_extalgo(
    &point, &surface_handle, extrema::ExtAlgo::Grad,
);

// Get closest distance
let dist = projector.lower_distance();  // f64

// Or project more points with .perform()
let another_point = gp::Pnt::new_real3(x2, y2, z2);
projector.perform(&another_point);
if projector.is_done() && projector.nb_points() > 0 {
    let dist2 = projector.lower_distance();
}
```

Real usage in the codebase at [crates/opencascade/src/primitives/face.rs](face.rs#L281):
```rust
let surface = b_rep::Tool::surface_face(&self.inner);
let projector = geom_api::ProjectPointOnSurf::new_pnt_handlegeomsurface_extalgo(
    &make_point(pos), &surface, extrema::ExtAlgo::Grad,
);
projector.lower_distance_parameters(&mut u, &mut v);
```

**Option B: `BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape`** — distance between two shapes (e.g., vertex to face):
```rust
use opencascade_sys::{b_rep_extrema, extrema, message};

let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
let mut dist = b_rep_extrema::DistShapeShape::new_shape2_extflag_extalgo_progressrange(
    &shape1, &shape2,
    extrema::ExtFlag::MinMax,  // or specific flag
    extrema::ExtAlgo::Grad,
    &progress,
);

if dist.is_done() {
    let min_dist = dist.value();                    // f64
    let pt1 = dist.point_on_shape1(1);             // &gp::Pnt
    let pt2 = dist.point_on_shape2(1);             // &gp::Pnt
    let n_solutions = dist.nb_solution();
}
```

### 5. Basic Geometry Types

**Module `gp`**:
```rust
use opencascade_sys::gp;

let p = gp::Pnt::new_real3(1.0, 2.0, 3.0);
let x = p.x();
let y = p.y();
let z = p.z();
let dist = p.distance(&other_point);

let v = gp::Vec::new_real3(1.0, 0.0, 0.0);
let d = gp::Dir::new_real3(0.0, 0.0, 1.0);
let ax1 = gp::Ax1::new_pnt_dir(&p, &d);
let ax2 = gp::Ax2::new_pnt_dir(&p, &d);
let trsf = gp::Trsf::new();
```

### 6. Accessing STL Mesh Data (Poly_Triangulation)

**Module `poly`** — `Poly_Triangulation` gives you nodes and triangles:
```rust
use opencascade_sys::poly;

// tri: &Poly_Triangulation (from rw_stl::read_file or BRep_Tool::triangulation)
let n_nodes = tri.nb_nodes();
let n_tris = tri.nb_triangles();

for i in 1..=n_nodes {
    let pt = tri.node(i);  // OwnedPtr<gp::Pnt>
    let x = pt.x();
    let y = pt.y();
    let z = pt.z();
}

for i in 1..=n_tris {
    let triangle = tri.triangle(i);  // &poly::Triangle
    let n1 = triangle.value(1);  // i32 (1-indexed node index)
    let n2 = triangle.value(2);
    let n3 = triangle.value(3);
}
```

## Summary for Your Use Case (STL vertex → STEP surface distance)

The full pipeline in `opencascade-sys` would be:

1. **Read STL**: `rw_stl::read_file_*` → `HandlePolyTriangulation`, or `stl_api::Reader` → `Shape`
2. **Read STEP**: `step_control::Reader` → transfer roots → `one_shape()` → `OwnedPtr<Shape>`
3. **Iterate STEP faces**: `top_exp::Explorer` with `ShapeEnum::Face`
4. **Extract surface**: `b_rep::Tool::surface_face(&face)` → `HandleGeomSurface`
5. **For each STL vertex**: `geom_api::ProjectPointOnSurf` → `lower_distance()`
6. Or use `b_rep_extrema::DistShapeShape` for shape-level distance computation

All the needed APIs are bound and available through `opencascade-sys`. No PORTING.md exists as a separate file — it's at [crates/opencascade-sys/PORTING.md](/Users/sethml/src/opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys/PORTING.md).

Here's the complete API reference from the generated bindings:

---

## Poly_Triangulation (`poly.rs`)

**Type:** `pub use crate::ffi::Poly_Triangulation as Triangulation;`

**Constructors:**
- `Triangulation::new()` — empty
- `Triangulation::new_int2_bool2(nbNodes, nbTriangles, hasUVNodes, hasNormals)` — pre-allocates
- `Triangulation::new_array1ofpnt_array1oftriangle(Nodes, Triangles)` — from arrays

**Reading data (all indices are 1-based):**
- `nb_nodes(&self) -> i32`
- `nb_triangles(&self) -> i32`
- `node(&self, theIndex: i32) -> OwnedPtr<gp::Pnt>` — returns a **copy** of the point
- `triangle(&self, theIndex: i32) -> &Triangle` — returns reference to triangle
- `has_normals(&self) -> bool`
- `normal_int(&self, theIndex: i32) -> OwnedPtr<gp::Dir>`
- `has_uv_nodes(&self) -> bool`
- `uv_node(&self, theIndex: i32) -> OwnedPtr<gp::Pnt2d>`
- `deflection(&self) -> f64`
- `compute_normals(&mut self)` — compute smooth normals by averaging

**Wrapping in Handle:**
```rust
let tri = Triangulation::new_int2_bool2(n, t, false, false);
let handle: OwnedPtr<HandlePolyTriangulation> = Triangulation::to_handle(tri);
```

## HandlePolyTriangulation

```rust
pub use crate::ffi::HandlePolyTriangulation;
```
- `get(&self) -> &Poly_Triangulation` — dereference to access the triangulation
- `get_mut(&mut self) -> &mut Poly_Triangulation`
- `to_handle_transient(&self) -> OwnedPtr<HandleStandardTransient>` — upcast

No `is_null` method on this handle type. The `OwnedPtr` wrapping handles null checks implicitly.

## Poly_Triangle

```rust
pub use crate::ffi::Poly_Triangle as Triangle;
```
- `get(&self, theN1: &mut i32, theN2: &mut i32, theN3: &mut i32)` — get all 3 node indices
- `value(&self, theIndex: i32) -> i32` — get one node index (theIndex ∈ {1, 2, 3})

## RWStl — STL Reading (`rw_stl.rs`)

**Best function for reading STL → `HandlePolyTriangulation`:**
```rust
// Takes &str path, returns OwnedPtr<HandlePolyTriangulation>
pub fn read_file_charptr_progressrange_2(
    theFile: &str,
    theProgress: &crate::message::ProgressRange,
) -> OwnedPtr<HandlePolyTriangulation>
```

**With merge angle control:**
```rust
pub fn read_file_charptr_real_progressrange(
    theFile: &str,
    theMergeAngle: f64,   // M_PI/2 = merge all
    theProgress: &crate::message::ProgressRange,
) -> OwnedPtr<HandlePolyTriangulation>
```

**Usage pattern:**
```rust
let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
let handle = rw_stl::read_file_charptr_progressrange_2("model.stl", &progress);
let tri = handle.get();  // &Poly_Triangulation
println!("nodes: {}, triangles: {}", tri.nb_nodes(), tri.nb_triangles());
```

**Writing STL** requires `&HandlePolyTriangulation` + `&osd::Path` + `&ProgressRange`.

## StlAPI (`stl_api.rs`)

**StlAPI_Reader** — reads STL into a `TopoDS_Shape` (each triangle → a face). **Documented as very inefficient for large files.** The source docs say: *"Consider reading STL file to Poly_Triangulation object instead (see class RWStl)."*

```rust
let mut reader = stl_api::Reader::new();
let mut shape = topo_ds::Shape::new();  // needs mutable
reader.read(&mut shape, "file.stl");    // returns bool
```

## BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape (`b_rep_extrema.rs`)

**Type:** `pub use crate::ffi::BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape as DistShapeShape;`

**Construction patterns:**

```rust
// Pattern 1: empty tool, load shapes, then perform
let mut dist = DistShapeShape::new();
dist.load_s1(&shape1);
dist.load_s2(&shape2);
let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
dist.perform(&progress); // returns bool (IsDone)

// Pattern 2: construct + compute immediately
let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
let dist = DistShapeShape::new_shape2_extflag_extalgo_progressrange(
    &shape1, &shape2,
    0,                       // F — obsolete, unused
    extrema::ExtAlgo::Grad,  // A — obsolete, unused
    &progress,
);
```

**Key result methods:**
- `is_done(&self) -> bool`
- `value(&self) -> f64` — minimum distance
- `nb_solution(&self) -> i32` — number of solution pairs
- `point_on_shape1(&self, N: i32) -> &gp::Pnt` — N is 1-based
- `point_on_shape2(&self, N: i32) -> &gp::Pnt`
- `inner_solution(&self) -> bool` — true if one solid contains the other
- `support_type_shape1(&self, N: i32) -> SupportType` — `Isvertex`/`Isonedge`/`Isinface`
- `support_on_shape1(&self, N: i32) -> OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Shape>`
- `par_on_face_s1(&self, N: i32, u: &mut f64, v: &mut f64)` — UV params if on face
- `set_multi_thread(&mut self, bool)` — enable parallel computation

## Supporting Types

**`message::ProgressRange`** — default empty constructor: `ProgressRange::new()`

**`extrema::ExtAlgo`** — `enum { Grad = 0, Tree = 1 }`

**`b_rep_extrema::SupportType`** — `enum { Isvertex = 0, Isonedge = 1, Isinface = 2 }`

</details>

```rust
pub use crate::ffi::BRepBuilderAPI_MakeVertex as MakeVertex;

impl MakeVertex {
    /// Constructs a vertex from point P.
    pub fn new_pnt(P: &crate::gp::Pnt) -> crate::OwnedPtr<Self>;

    /// Returns the constructed vertex.
    pub fn vertex(&mut self) -> &crate::topo_ds::Vertex;

    // inherited:
    pub fn as_make_shape(&self) -> &MakeShape;
    pub fn as_make_shape_mut(&mut self) -> &mut MakeShape;
}
```

### 2. `b_rep::Tool` (BRep_Tool) — Surface extraction

```rust
pub use crate::ffi::BRep_Tool as Tool;

impl Tool {
    /// Returns the geometric surface of the face (may be a copy if there is a Location).
    pub fn surface_face(
        F: &crate::topo_ds::Face,
    ) -> crate::OwnedPtr<crate::ffi::HandleGeomSurface>;

    /// Returns the geometric surface of the face + location (no copy).
    pub fn surface_face_location(
        F: &crate::topo_ds::Face,
        L: &mut crate::top_loc::Location,
    ) -> &'static crate::ffi::HandleGeomSurface;
}
```

### 3. `geom_api::ProjectPointOnSurf`

```rust
pub use crate::ffi::GeomAPI_ProjectPointOnSurf as ProjectPointOnSurf;

impl ProjectPointOnSurf {
    /// Empty constructor — use Init() afterwards.
    pub fn new() -> crate::OwnedPtr<Self>;

    /// Point + Surface + algorithm.
    pub fn new_pnt_handlegeomsurface_extalgo(
        P: &crate::gp::Pnt,
        Surface: &crate::ffi::HandleGeomSurface,
        Algo: crate::extrema::ExtAlgo,
    ) -> crate::OwnedPtr<Self>;

    /// Point + Surface + tolerance + algorithm.
    pub fn new_pnt_handlegeomsurface_real_extalgo(
        P: &crate::gp::Pnt,
        Surface: &crate::ffi::HandleGeomSurface,
        Tolerance: f64,
        Algo: crate::extrema::ExtAlgo,
    ) -> crate::OwnedPtr<Self>;

    // Also: variants with UV bounds (real4, real5)

    pub fn perform(&mut self, P: &crate::gp::Pnt);
    pub fn is_done(&self) -> bool;
    pub fn nb_points(&self) -> i32;
    pub fn point(&self, Index: i32) -> crate::OwnedPtr<crate::gp::Pnt>;
    pub fn parameters(&self, Index: i32, U: &mut f64, V: &mut f64);
    pub fn distance(&self, Index: i32) -> f64;
    pub fn nearest_point(&self) -> crate::OwnedPtr<crate::gp::Pnt>;
    pub fn lower_distance_parameters(&self, U: &mut f64, V: &mut f64);
    pub fn lower_distance(&self) -> f64;
}
```

`ExtAlgo` enum:
```rust
pub enum ExtAlgo { Grad = 0, Tree = 1 }
```

### 4. `topo_ds::face_shape` (free function, Shape→Face downcast)

```rust
/// Casts shape theShape to the more specialized return type, Face.
/// @throws Standard_TypeMismatch if theShape cannot be cast.
pub fn face_shape(theShape: &Shape) -> &Face;

pub fn face_mut(theShape: &mut Shape) -> &mut Face;
```

### Required imports

```rust
use opencascade_sys::b_rep_builder_api::MakeVertex;
use opencascade_sys::b_rep::Tool;                         // BRep_Tool
use opencascade_sys::geom_api::ProjectPointOnSurf;
use opencascade_sys::topo_ds::{self, Shape, Face};
use opencascade_sys::gp::Pnt;
use opencascade_sys::extrema::ExtAlgo;
use opencascade_sys::ffi::HandleGeomSurface;              // opaque handle type from ffi
use opencascade_sys::top_loc::Location;                   // if using surface_face_location
```

The `HandleGeomSurface` is an opaque struct defined in `ffi.rs` (line 2778) and re-exported in `geom.rs` (line 21441). It flows directly from `Tool::surface_face(&Face) -> OwnedPtr<HandleGeomSurface>` into `ProjectPointOnSurf::new_pnt_handlegeomsurface_extalgo(&Pnt, &HandleGeomSurface, ExtAlgo)`.

Phase 1 is complete. Here's a summary:

**Project setup:**
- `Cargo.toml` — Rust crate depending on `opencascade-sys` (path dep to `/Users/sethml/src/opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys`)
- `src/lib.rs` — placeholder library crate for future stages
- Added `target/` to `.gitignore`

**Test utility** (`src/bin/step_distance.rs`):
- Reads STL via `rw_stl` → `Poly_Triangulation` (vertex extraction)
- Reads STEP via `step_control::Reader` → `TopoDS_Shape`
- Extracts geometric surfaces from all STEP faces via `TopExp_Explorer` + `BRep_Tool::Surface`
- Projects each STL vertex onto every STEP surface via `GeomAPI_ProjectPointOnSurf`, takes minimum distance
- Reports max and average distance to stderr, max distance to stdout

**Script** (`scripts/step_distance_table.sh`):
- Finds all `.stl`/`.step` pairs under `tests/`
- Runs `step-distance` on each pair
- Prints a formatted table

# Instructions for LLM Coding Assistants

This file contains guidelines for AI/LLM coding assistants working on this project.

## Commit Messages

All commit messages must include:

1. **A clear description** of the changes made
2. **The AI agent/model** used to generate the changes (e.g., "Claude Sonnet 4 (Anthropic, 2025)")
3. **The user prompts** that led to the changes

Format:
```
<descriptive commit title>

<detailed description of changes>

---
Generated with assistance from <model name> (<provider>, <year>)

User prompt(s):
- "<prompt 1>"
- "<prompt 2>"
  (include context for prompts that reference previous options/decisions)
```

**Shell Quoting**: Use heredoc quoting for commit messages and when writing test scripts to avoid shell parsing issues:

```bash
# Good: heredoc (recommended)
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
Fix UV bounds for spheres

- Detailed change 1
- Detailed change 2

---
Generated with assistance from Model Name (Provider, 2026)

User prompt(s):
- 'Prompt with "quotes" and other special chars'
EOF
```

## Development Plan Maintenance

Keep DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md up to date with each commit:

1. **Mark completed items** - Check off (`[x]`) any Implementation Phases items that are completed by the commit
2. **Add new tasks** - If work reveals new tasks or sub-tasks not in the plan, add them to the appropriate phase
3. **Modify existing tasks** - If a planned approach changes (e.g., different algorithm, new dependency), update the relevant sections
4. **Include in commit** - Changes to DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md should be part of the same commit as the implementation work

## README Maintenance

Keep README.md up to date when changes affect user-facing behavior:

1. **New dependencies** - Add to the dependencies list with installation instructions
2. **New CLI options** - Update the usage examples and options documentation
3. **Changed defaults** - Document any changes to default parameter values
4. **New features** - Add brief descriptions of significant new capabilities
5. **Include in commit** - README updates should be part of the same commit as the feature work

## File Editing

When reading files for editing, use `#tool:hashlineRead` instead of the
built-in file read tool. It returns lines tagged with content hashes in the
format `{lineNumber}:{hash}|{content}`.

When editing files, use `#tool:hashlineEdit` instead of string-replace tools.
Reference lines by their `{line}:{hash}` pairs from the read output. This
avoids needing to reproduce existing file content and prevents edits to stale
files.

Example workflow:
1. Read: `hashline_read({filePath: "src/app.ts", startLine: 1, endLine: 20})`
   Returns: `1:qk|import React...`
2. Edit: `hashline_edit({edits: [{filePath: "src/app.ts", lineHashes: "4:mp", content: "  return <div>Hello</div>;"}]})`

Operations:
- **Replace**: set `lineHashes` to all lines being replaced, `content` to new text
- **Insert after**: set `insertAfter: true`, `lineHashes` to anchor line
- **Delete**: set `content` to empty string
- Multiple edits can be batched in one call across files

### Don't Truncate Build or Test Output

Never pipe build or test commands through `head`, `tail`, or other truncating filters:
- Errors often appear at unexpected locations in the output
- Truncating can hide the actual failure while showing misleading context
- Build systems and test frameworks already produce focused error output

### Time Builds and Tests

Run builds and tests with `time`. When they take more than 5 minutes, stop and ask the user whether to speed them up.

## Temporary Test Code

When writing temporary code for debugging or testing:

1. **Put files in `tmp/`** - This directory is in the project root and ignored by git. Don't use `/tmp` or other directories outside the workspace.

2. **Don't delete temporary code** - Leave it in `tmp/` in case it's useful later.

```
/// Compute maximum distance between STL mesh vertices and STEP model surfaces.
///
/// Reads an STL file to extract vertex positions, reads a STEP file to extract
/// surfaces, and computes the minimum distance from each STL vertex to any STEP
/// surface. Reports the maximum such distance (i.e., the worst-case vertex error).
use opencascade_sys::{
    b_rep, extrema, geom_api, message, rw_stl, step_control, top_abs, top_exp, topo_ds,
};
use std::env;
use std::process;

fn main() {
    let args: Vec<String> = env::args().collect();
    if args.len() != 3 {
        eprintln!("Usage: {} <input.stl> <input.step>", args[0]);
        process::exit(1);
    }
    let stl_path = &args[1];
    let step_path = &args[2];

    // Read STL → Poly_Triangulation
    let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
    let tri_handle = rw_stl::read_file_charptr_progressrange_2(stl_path, &progress);
    let tri = tri_handle.get();
    let num_nodes = tri.nb_nodes();
    let num_triangles = tri.nb_triangles();
    eprintln!("STL: {} nodes, {} triangles", num_nodes, num_triangles);

    if num_nodes == 0 {
        eprintln!("Error: STL file has no vertices");
        process::exit(1);
    }

    // Read STEP
    let mut reader = step_control::Reader::new();
    reader.read_file_charptr(step_path);
    let progress2 = message::ProgressRange::new();
    reader.transfer_roots(&progress2);
    let step_shape = reader.one_shape();

    // Collect surfaces from all STEP faces
    let mut surfaces = Vec::new();
    let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
        &step_shape,
        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
    );
    while explorer.more() {
        let shape_ref = explorer.value();
        let face = topo_ds::face_shape(shape_ref);
        surfaces.push(b_rep::Tool::surface_face(face));
        explorer.next();
    }
    eprintln!("STEP: {} faces", surfaces.len());

    if surfaces.is_empty() {
        eprintln!("Error: STEP file has no faces");
        process::exit(1);
    }

    // For each STL vertex, find minimum distance to any STEP surface
    let mut max_dist = 0.0_f64;
    let mut max_vertex_idx = 1_i32;
    let mut dist_sum = 0.0_f64;

    for i in 1..=num_nodes {
        let pt = tri.node(i);
        let mut min_dist = f64::MAX;

        for surface in &surfaces {
            let projector = geom_api::ProjectPointOnSurf::new_pnt_handlegeomsurface_extalgo(
                &pt,
                surface,
                extrema::ExtAlgo::Grad,
            );
            if projector.is_done() && projector.nb_points() > 0 {
                let d = projector.lower_distance();
                if d < min_dist {
                    min_dist = d;
                }
            }
        }

        if min_dist < f64::MAX {
            dist_sum += min_dist;
            if min_dist > max_dist {
                max_dist = min_dist;
                max_vertex_idx = i;
            }
        }
    }

    let avg_dist = dist_sum / num_nodes as f64;
    let worst_pt = tri.node(max_vertex_idx);
    eprintln!(
        "Worst vertex #{}: ({:.6}, {:.6}, {:.6})",
        max_vertex_idx,
        worst_pt.x(),
        worst_pt.y(),
        worst_pt.z()
    );
    eprintln!("Average distance: {:.10}", avg_dist);
    eprintln!("Maximum distance: {:.10}", max_dist);

    // Print max distance to stdout for scripting
    println!("{:.10}", max_dist);
}

```

```
#!/bin/bash
# Run step-distance on all STL/STEP pairs under tests/ and print a table.
set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
PROJECT_DIR="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
BINARY="$PROJECT_DIR/target/release/step-distance"

# Build release binary
echo "Building step-distance (release)..." >&2
cargo build --release --bin step-distance --manifest-path "$PROJECT_DIR/Cargo.toml" 2>&1 | tail -1 >&2

# Print header
printf "%-45s %15s %15s %8s %8s\n" "Test Case" "Max Distance" "Avg Distance" "Nodes" "Faces"
printf "%-45s %15s %15s %8s %8s\n" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..45})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..15})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..15})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})"

# Find all STL files and look for matching STEP files
find "$PROJECT_DIR/tests" -name "*.stl" -not -name "*.ascii.stl" | sort | while read -r stl_file; do
    step_file="${stl_file%.stl}.step"
    if [ -f "$step_file" ]; then
        rel_path="${stl_file#"$PROJECT_DIR"/tests/}"
        base="${rel_path%.stl}"

        # Run tool, capture stderr for diagnostics and stdout for max distance
        diag_file=$(mktemp)
        max_dist=$("$BINARY" "$stl_file" "$step_file" 2>"$diag_file") || {
            printf "%-45s %15s\n" "$base" "ERROR"
            rm -f "$diag_file"
            continue
        }

        # Parse diagnostic output
        nodes=$(grep "^STL:" "$diag_file" | sed 's/STL: \([0-9]*\) nodes.*/\1/')
        faces=$(grep "^STEP:" "$diag_file" | sed 's/STEP: \([0-9]*\) faces/\1/')
        avg_dist=$(grep "^Average" "$diag_file" | sed 's/Average distance: //')
        rm -f "$diag_file"

        printf "%-45s %15s %15s %8s %8s\n" "$base" "$max_dist" "$avg_dist" "$nodes" "$faces"
    fi
done

```

```
#!/bin/bash
# Run step-distance on all STL/STEP pairs under tests/ and print a table.
set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
PROJECT_DIR="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
BINARY="$PROJECT_DIR/target/release/step-distance"

# Build release binary
echo "Building step-distance (release)..." >&2
cargo build --release --bin step-distance --manifest-path "$PROJECT_DIR/Cargo.toml" 2>&1 | tail -1 >&2

# Print header
printf "%-45s %15s %15s %8s %8s\n" "Test Case" "Max Distance" "Avg Distance" "Nodes" "Faces"
printf "%-45s %15s %15s %8s %8s\n" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..45})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..15})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..15})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})"

# Find all STL files and look for matching STEP files
find "$PROJECT_DIR/tests" -name "*.stl" -not -name "*.ascii.stl" | sort | while read -r stl_file; do
    step_file="${stl_file%.stl}.step"
    if [ -f "$step_file" ]; then
        rel_path="${stl_file#"$PROJECT_DIR"/tests/}"
        base="${rel_path%.stl}"

        # Run tool, capture stderr for diagnostics and stdout for max distance
        diag_file=$(mktemp)
        max_dist=$("$BINARY" "$stl_file" "$step_file" 2>"$diag_file") || {
            printf "%-45s %15s\n" "$base" "ERROR"
            rm -f "$diag_file"
            continue
        }

        # Parse diagnostic output
        nodes=$(grep "^STL:" "$diag_file" | sed 's/STL: \([0-9]*\) nodes.*/\1/')
        faces=$(grep "^STEP:" "$diag_file" | sed 's/STEP: \([0-9]*\) faces/\1/')
        avg_dist=$(grep "^Average" "$diag_file" | sed 's/Average distance: //')
        rm -f "$diag_file"

        printf "%-45s %15s %15s %8s %8s\n" "$base" "$max_dist" "$avg_dist" "$nodes" "$faces"
    fi
done
```

```sh
Add triangle centroid distance tracking to step-distance

Compute the centroid (average of 3 vertices) of each STL triangle and
measure its distance to the nearest STEP surface, tracking max and average
separately from vertex distances. Centroid error measures chord deviation
(how far the flat triangle interior is from the true surface), which is
larger than vertex error for curved surfaces.

Changes:
- src/bin/step_distance.rs: extract helper closure for surface distance
  computation, add centroid loop over all triangles, report both vertex
  and centroid stats on stderr, output tab-separated pair on stdout
- scripts/step_distance_table.sh: parse both distance values and display
  4 distance columns (vertex max/avg, centroid max/avg)
```

```
/// Compute maximum distance between STL mesh vertices/centroids and STEP model surfaces.
///
/// Reads an STL file to extract vertex positions and triangle centroids, reads a
/// STEP file to extract surfaces, and computes the minimum distance from each
/// point to any STEP surface. Reports max distance for both vertices and centroids.
use opencascade_sys::{
    b_rep, extrema, geom_api, gp, message, rw_stl, step_control, top_abs, top_exp, topo_ds,
};
use std::env;
use std::process;

fn main() {
    let args: Vec<String> = env::args().collect();
    if args.len() != 3 {
        eprintln!("Usage: {} <input.stl> <input.step>", args[0]);
        process::exit(1);
    }
    let stl_path = &args[1];
    let step_path = &args[2];

    // Read STL → Poly_Triangulation
    let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
    let tri_handle = rw_stl::read_file_charptr_progressrange_2(stl_path, &progress);
    let tri = tri_handle.get();
    let num_nodes = tri.nb_nodes();
    let num_triangles = tri.nb_triangles();
    eprintln!("STL: {} nodes, {} triangles", num_nodes, num_triangles);

    if num_nodes == 0 {
        eprintln!("Error: STL file has no vertices");
        process::exit(1);
    }

    // Read STEP
    let mut reader = step_control::Reader::new();
    reader.read_file_charptr(step_path);
    let progress2 = message::ProgressRange::new();
    reader.transfer_roots(&progress2);
    let step_shape = reader.one_shape();

    // Collect surfaces from all STEP faces
    let mut surfaces = Vec::new();
    let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
        &step_shape,
        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
    );
    while explorer.more() {
        let shape_ref = explorer.value();
        let face = topo_ds::face_shape(shape_ref);
        surfaces.push(b_rep::Tool::surface_face(face));
        explorer.next();
    }
    eprintln!("STEP: {} faces", surfaces.len());

    if surfaces.is_empty() {
        eprintln!("Error: STEP file has no faces");
        process::exit(1);
    }

    // Helper: find minimum distance from a point to any STEP surface
    let min_distance_to_surfaces = |pt: &gp::Pnt| -> f64 {
        let mut min_dist = f64::MAX;
        for surface in &surfaces {
            let projector = geom_api::ProjectPointOnSurf::new_pnt_handlegeomsurface_extalgo(
                pt,
                surface,
                extrema::ExtAlgo::Grad,
            );
            if projector.is_done() && projector.nb_points() > 0 {
                let d = projector.lower_distance();
                if d < min_dist {
                    min_dist = d;
                }
            }
        }
        min_dist
    };

    // Vertex distances
    let mut vtx_max_dist = 0.0_f64;
    let mut vtx_max_idx = 1_i32;
    let mut vtx_dist_sum = 0.0_f64;

    for i in 1..=num_nodes {
        let pt = tri.node(i);
        let min_dist = min_distance_to_surfaces(&pt);
        if min_dist < f64::MAX {
            vtx_dist_sum += min_dist;
            if min_dist > vtx_max_dist {
                vtx_max_dist = min_dist;
                vtx_max_idx = i;
            }
        }
    }

    // Centroid distances
    let mut ctr_max_dist = 0.0_f64;
    let mut _ctr_max_idx = 1_i32;
    let mut ctr_dist_sum = 0.0_f64;

    for i in 1..=num_triangles {
        let triangle = tri.triangle(i);
        let mut n1 = 0_i32;
        let mut n2 = 0_i32;
        let mut n3 = 0_i32;
        triangle.get(&mut n1, &mut n2, &mut n3);
        let p1 = tri.node(n1);
        let p2 = tri.node(n2);
        let p3 = tri.node(n3);
        let centroid = gp::Pnt::new_real3(
            (p1.x() + p2.x() + p3.x()) / 3.0,
            (p1.y() + p2.y() + p3.y()) / 3.0,
            (p1.z() + p2.z() + p3.z()) / 3.0,
        );
        let min_dist = min_distance_to_surfaces(&centroid);
        if min_dist < f64::MAX {
            ctr_dist_sum += min_dist;
            if min_dist > ctr_max_dist {
                ctr_max_dist = min_dist;
```

```
#!/bin/bash
# Run step-distance on all STL/STEP pairs under tests/ and print a table.
set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
PROJECT_DIR="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
BINARY="$PROJECT_DIR/target/release/step-distance"

# Build release binary
echo "Building step-distance (release)..." >&2
cargo build --release --bin step-distance --manifest-path "$PROJECT_DIR/Cargo.toml" 2>&1 | tail -1 >&2

# Print header
printf "%-40s %15s %15s %15s %15s %8s %8s\n" "Test Case" "Vtx Max Dist" "Vtx Avg Dist" "Ctr Max Dist" "Ctr Avg Dist" "Nodes" "Faces"
printf "%-40s %15s %15s %15s %15s %8s %8s\n" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..40})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..15})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..15})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..15})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..15})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})" \
    "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})"

# Find all STL files and look for matching STEP files
find "$PROJECT_DIR/tests" -name "*.stl" -not -name "*.ascii.stl" | sort | while read -r stl_file; do
    step_file="${stl_file%.stl}.step"
    if [ -f "$step_file" ]; then
        rel_path="${stl_file#"$PROJECT_DIR"/tests/}"
        base="${rel_path%.stl}"

        # Run tool, capture stderr for diagnostics and stdout for distances
        diag_file=$(mktemp)
        output=$($BINARY "$stl_file" "$step_file" 2>"$diag_file") || {
            printf "%-40s %15s\n" "$base" "ERROR"
            rm -f "$diag_file"
            continue
        }

        # Parse stdout (tab-separated: vtx_max_dist ctr_max_dist)
        vtx_max_dist=$(echo "$output" | cut -f1)
        ctr_max_dist=$(echo "$output" | cut -f2)

        # Parse diagnostic output
        nodes=$(grep "^STL:" "$diag_file" | sed 's/STL: \([0-9]*\) nodes.*/\1/')
        faces=$(grep "^STEP:" "$diag_file" | sed 's/STEP: \([0-9]*\) faces/\1/')
        vtx_avg_dist=$(grep "^Vertex" "$diag_file" | sed 's/.*avg: \([^ ]*\).*/\1/')
        ctr_avg_dist=$(grep "^Centroid" "$diag_file" | sed 's/.*avg: \([^ ]*\).*/\1/')
        rm -f "$diag_file"

        printf "%-40s %15s %15s %15s %15s %8s %8s\n" "$base" "$vtx_max_dist" "$vtx_avg_dist" "$ctr_max_dist" "$ctr_avg_dist" "$nodes" "$faces"
    fi
done
```

The sphere uses coordinates like -0.0125 (meters) — the "25" in "sphere_25" is 25mm = 0.025m diameter, so radius 0.0125m. OCCT's `STEPControl_Reader` applies the unit conversion, so coordinates are in the STEP file's declared units. Since files use different units (meters vs millimeters), OCCT reads them using whatever unit the file declares — meaning coordinates could be in meters OR millimeters depending on the file.

Let me verify what OCCT actually gives us:

The sphere vertex at (8.4, 8.4, 3.8) — that's millimeters (the STL doesn't have unit info, it's just raw coordinates in mm). The STEP coordinates are in meters but the STL coordinates are not. So OCCT's `STEPControl_Reader` is exposing coordinates in the file's original units. Effectively we have mixed units depending on file source, but within one file pair the STL and STEP are consistent (both are in whatever unit the CAD tool used).

Since these are Onshape exports, the STL vertices are in mm. The units are file-dependent. Best approach: compute bounding box dimension from the STEP model and report it, and refer to distances as being "in model units" (since files may vary). Let me now implement all the changes:

Reports max distance for both vertices and centroids.\n///\n/// Units: distances are in the same units as the STEP file (typically mm).\nuse opencascade_sys::{\n    b_rep, extrema, geom_api, gp, message, rw_stl, step_control, top_abs, top_exp, topo_ds,\n};",

```
      "content": "\n    // Compute STL bounding box and max dimension\n    let mut bb_min = [f64::MAX; 3];\n    let mut bb_max = [f64::MIN; 3];\n    for i in 1..=num_nodes {\n        let pt = tri.node(i);\n        let coords = [pt.x(), pt.y(), pt.z()];\n        for d in 0..3 {\n            bb_min[d] = bb_min[d].min(coords[d]);\n            bb_max[d] = bb_max[d].max(coords[d]);\n        }\n    }\n    let extents = [\n        bb_max[0] - bb_min[0],\n        bb_max[1] - bb_min[1],\n        bb_max[2] - bb_min[2],\n    ];\n    let max_dimension = extents[0].max(extents[1]).max(extents[2]);\n    eprintln!(\"Bounding box: {:.6} x {:.6} x {:.6}\", extents[0], extents[1], extents[2]);\n    eprintln!(\"Max dimension: {:.6}\", max_dimension);\n",
```

Wait — there's a problem. The `while read` loop runs in a subshell due to the pipe from `find`, so associative arrays won't persist. Let me restructure the script to avoid that issue:

<details>
<summary>Reading all lines of <a href="../scripts/step_distance_table.sh">step_distance_table.sh</a></summary>

```
#!/bin/bash
# Run step-distance on all STL/STEP pairs under tests/ and print a table
# with per-group (suffix) summary rows.
set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
PROJECT_DIR="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
BINARY="$PROJECT_DIR/target/release/step-distance"

# Build release binary
echo "Building step-distance (release)..." >&2
cargo build --release --bin step-distance --manifest-path "$PROJECT_DIR/Cargo.toml" 2>&1 | tail -1 >&2

# Format: columns are test case, vtx max, vtx avg, ctr max, ctr avg, max dim, nodes, faces
# All distances in microns (µm). Max dimension in model units.
HDR_FMT="%-40s %12s %12s %12s %12s %10s %8s %8s\n"
ROW_FMT="%-40s %12s %12s %12s %12s %10s %8s %8s\n"
SEP_FMT="%-40s %12s %12s %12s %12s %10s %8s %8s\n"

print_header() {
    printf "$HDR_FMT" "Test Case" "Vtx Max µm" "Vtx Avg µm" "Ctr Max µm" "Ctr Avg µm" "Max Dim" "Nodes" "Faces"
    printf "$SEP_FMT" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..40})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..10})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})"
}

# Convert scientific notation value to microns, formatted as e.g. "0.13e-6" style
# Input: value in model units. Output: value * 1e6 in scientific notation.
to_microns() {
    awk "BEGIN { v = $1 * 1e6; if (v == 0) printf \"0\"; else printf \"%.2e\", v }"
}

# Track max values per group for summary rows
declare -A group_vtx_max group_vtx_avg group_ctr_max group_ctr_avg

update_group_max() {
    local group="$1" vtx_max="$2" vtx_avg="$3" ctr_max="$4" ctr_avg="$5"
    if [ -z "${group_vtx_max[$group]:-}" ]; then
        group_vtx_max[$group]="$vtx_max"
        group_vtx_avg[$group]="$vtx_avg"
        group_ctr_max[$group]="$ctr_max"
        group_ctr_avg[$group]="$ctr_avg"
    else
        group_vtx_max[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_vtx_max[$group]}; b=$vtx_max; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
        group_vtx_avg[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_vtx_avg[$group]}; b=$vtx_avg; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
        group_ctr_max[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_ctr_max[$group]}; b=$ctr_max; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
        group_ctr_avg[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_ctr_avg[$group]}; b=$ctr_avg; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
    fi
}

print_group_summary() {
    local group="$1"
    if [ -n "${group_vtx_max[$group]:-}" ]; then
        local vm=$(to_microns "${group_vtx_max[$group]}")
        local va=$(to_microns "${group_vtx_avg[$group]}")
        local cm=$(to_microns "${group_ctr_max[$group]}")
        local ca=$(to_microns "${group_ctr_avg[$group]}")
        printf "$ROW_FMT" "  ** MAX ($group) **" "$vm" "$va" "$cm" "$ca" "" "" ""
    fi
}

# Collect results per directory, grouped by suffix
prev_dir=""
prev_groups=""

# Process all STL/STEP pairs
print_header

find "$PROJECT_DIR/tests" -name "*.stl" -not -name "*.ascii.stl" | sort | while read -r stl_file; do
    step_file="${stl_file%.stl}.step"
    if [ -f "$step_file" ]; then
        rel_path="${stl_file#"$PROJECT_DIR"/tests/}"
        base="${rel_path%.stl}"
        dir=$(dirname "$base")

        # Detect directory change and print group summaries
        if [ "$dir" != "$prev_dir" ] && [ -n "$prev_dir" ]; then
            for g in $prev_groups; do
                print_group_summary "$g"
            done
            echo
            group_vtx_max=(); group_vtx_avg=(); group_ctr_max=(); group_ctr_avg=()
            prev_groups=""
        fi
        prev_dir="$dir"

        # Extract group suffix (after last _)
        filename=$(basename "$base")
        group=$(echo "$filename" | sed 's/.*_//')

        # Run tool
        diag_file=$(mktemp)
        output=$("$BINARY" "$stl_file" "$step_file" 2>"$diag_file") || {
            printf "$ROW_FMT" "$base" "ERROR" "" "" "" "" "" ""
            rm -f "$diag_file"
            continue
        }
        rm -f "$diag_file"

        # Parse tab-se

```
```
#!/bin/bash
# Run step-distance on all STL/STEP pairs under tests/ and print a table
# with per-group (suffix) summary rows.
# Distances displayed in microns (µm). Max dimension in model units.
set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
PROJECT_DIR="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
BINARY="$PROJECT_DIR/target/release/step-distance"

# Build release binary
echo "Building step-distance (release)..." >&2
cargo build --release --bin step-distance --manifest-path "$PROJECT_DIR/Cargo.toml" 2>&1 | tail -1 >&2

ROW_FMT="%-40s %12s %12s %12s %12s %10s %8s %8s\n"

print_header() {
    printf "$ROW_FMT" "Test Case" "Vtx Max µm" "Vtx Avg µm" "Ctr Max µm" "Ctr Avg µm" "Max Dim" "Nodes" "Faces"
    print_sep
}

print_sep() {
    printf "$ROW_FMT" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..40})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..10})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})"
}

# Convert value to microns for display
to_microns() {
    awk "BEGIN { v = $1 * 1e6; if (v == 0) printf \"0\"; else printf \"%.2e\", v }"
}

# Collect all STL files into an array (avoids subshell from pipe)
stl_files=()
while IFS= read -r f; do stl_files+=("$f"); done < <(find "$PROJECT_DIR/tests" -name "*.stl" -not -name "*.ascii.stl" | sort)

# Track per-group maximums
declare -A group_vtx_max group_vtx_avg group_ctr_max group_ctr_avg
prev_dir=""
group_order=()

flush_groups() {
    if [ ${#group_order[@]} -gt 0 ]; then
        for g in "${group_order[@]}"; do
            local vm=$(to_microns "${group_vtx_max[$g]}")
            local va=$(to_microns "${group_vtx_avg[$g]}")
            local cm=$(to_microns "${group_ctr_max[$g]}")
            local ca=$(to_microns "${group_ctr_avg[$g]}")
            printf "$ROW_FMT" "  ** MAX ($g) **" "$vm" "$va" "$cm" "$ca" "" "" ""
        done
    fi
    group_vtx_max=(); group_vtx_avg=(); group_ctr_max=(); group_ctr_avg=()
    group_order=()
}

update_group_max() {
    local group="$1" vm="$2" va="$3" cm="$4" ca="$5"
    if [ -z "${group_vtx_max[$group]:-}" ]; then
        group_vtx_max[$group]="$vm"
        group_vtx_avg[$group]="$va"
        group_ctr_max[$group]="$cm"
        group_ctr_avg[$group]="$ca"
        group_order+=("$group")
    else
        group_vtx_max[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_vtx_max[$group]}; b=$vm; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
        group_vtx_avg[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_vtx_avg[$group]}; b=$va; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
        group_ctr_max[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_ctr_max[$group]}; b=$cm; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
        group_ctr_avg[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_ctr_avg[$group]}; b=$ca; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
    fi
}

print_header

for stl_file in "${stl_files[@]}"; do
    step_file="${stl_file%.stl}.step"
    [ -f "$step_file" ] || continue

    rel_path="${stl_file#"$PROJECT_DIR"/tests/}"
    base="${rel_path%.stl}"
    dir=$(dirname "$base")

    # On directory change, print group summaries
    if [ "$dir" != "$prev_dir" ] && [ -n "$prev_dir" ]; then
        flush_groups
        echo
    fi
    prev_dir="$dir"

    # Extract group suffix (after last _)
    filename=$(basename "$base")
    group=$(echo "$filename" | sed 's/.*_//')

    # Run tool, capture both stdout and stderr
    diag_file=$(mktemp)
    output=$("$BINARY" "$stl_file" "$step_file" 2>"$diag_file") || {
        printf "$ROW_FMT" "$base" "ERROR" "" "" "" "" "" ""
        rm -f "$diag_file"
        continue
    }

    # Parse tab-separated stdout: vtx_max vtx_avg ctr_max ctr_avg max_dim
    vtx_max_raw=$(echo "$output" | cut -f1)
    vtx_avg_raw=$(echo "$output" | cut -f2)
    ctr_max_raw=$(echo "$output" | cut -f3)
    ctr_avg_raw=$(echo "$output" | cut -f4)
    max_dim=$(echo "$output" | cut -f5)

    # Parse stderr for node/face counts
    nodes=$(grep "^STL:" "$diag_file" | sed 's/STL: \([0-9]*\) nodes.*/\1/')
    faces=$(gre
... (truncated, 4491 chars)
```

```
#!/bin/zsh

# Run step-distance on all STL/STEP pairs under tests/ and print a table
# with per-group (suffix) summary rows.
# Distances displayed in microns (µm). Max dimension in model units.
set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
PROJECT_DIR="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
BINARY="$PROJECT_DIR/target/release/step-distance"

# Build release binary
echo "Building step-distance (release)..." >&2
cargo build --release --bin step-distance --manifest-path "$PROJECT_DIR/Cargo.toml" 2>&1 | tail -1 >&2

ROW_FMT="%-40s %12s %12s %12s %12s %10s %8s %8s\n"

print_header() {
    printf "$ROW_FMT" "Test Case" "Vtx Max µm" "Vtx Avg µm" "Ctr Max µm" "Ctr Avg µm" "Max Dim" "Nodes" "Faces"
    print_sep
}

print_sep() {
    printf "$ROW_FMT" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..40})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..12})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..10})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})" \
        "$(printf '%0.s-' {1..8})"
}

# Convert value to microns for display
to_microns() {
    awk "BEGIN { v = $1 * 1e6; if (v == 0) printf \"0\"; else printf \"%.2f\", v }"
}

# Collect all STL files into an array (avoids subshell from pipe)
stl_files=(${(f)"$(find "$PROJECT_DIR/tests" -name '*.stl' -not -name '*.ascii.stl' | sort)"})

# Track per-group maximums
typeset -A group_vtx_max group_vtx_avg group_ctr_max group_ctr_avg
prev_dir=""
group_order=()

flush_groups() {
    if [[ ${#group_order[@]} -gt 0 ]]; then
        for g in "${group_order[@]}"; do
            local vm=$(to_microns "${group_vtx_max[$g]}")
            local va=$(to_microns "${group_vtx_avg[$g]}")
            local cm=$(to_microns "${group_ctr_max[$g]}")
            local ca=$(to_microns "${group_ctr_avg[$g]}")
            printf "$ROW_FMT" "  ** MAX ($g) **" "$vm" "$va" "$cm" "$ca" "" "" ""
        done
    fi
    typeset -A group_vtx_max group_vtx_avg group_ctr_max group_ctr_avg
    group_order=()
}

update_group_max() {
    local group="$1" vm="$2" va="$3" cm="$4" ca="$5"
    if [[ -z "${group_vtx_max[$group]:-}" ]]; then
        group_vtx_max[$group]="$vm"
        group_vtx_avg[$group]="$va"
        group_ctr_max[$group]="$cm"
        group_ctr_avg[$group]="$ca"
        group_order+=("$group")
    else
        group_vtx_max[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_vtx_max[$group]}; b=$vm; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
        group_vtx_avg[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_vtx_avg[$group]}; b=$va; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
        group_ctr_max[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_ctr_max[$group]}; b=$cm; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
        group_ctr_avg[$group]=$(awk "BEGIN { a=${group_ctr_avg[$group]}; b=$ca; print (a>b) ? a : b }")
    fi
}

print_header

for stl_file in "${stl_files[@]}"; do
    step_file="${stl_file%.stl}.step"
    [ -f "$step_file" ] || continue

    rel_path="${stl_file#${PROJECT_DIR}/tests/}"
    base="${rel_path%.stl}"
    dir=$(dirname "$base")

    # On directory change, print group summaries
    if [ "$dir" != "$prev_dir" ] && [ -n "$prev_dir" ]; then
        flush_groups
        echo
    fi
    prev_dir="$dir"

    # Extract group suffix (after last _)
    filename=$(basename "$base")
    group="${filename##*_}"

    # Run tool, capture both stdout and stderr
    diag_file=$(mktemp)
    output=$("$BINARY" "$stl_file" "$step_file" 2>"$diag_file") || {
        printf "$ROW_FMT" "$base" "ERROR" "" "" "" "" "" ""
        rm -f "$diag_file"
        continue
    }

    # Parse tab-separated stdout: vtx_max vtx_avg ctr_max ctr_avg max_dim
    vtx_max_raw=$(echo "$output" | cut -f1)
    vtx_avg_raw=$(echo "$output" | cut -f2)
    ctr_max_raw=$(echo "$output" | cut -f3)
    ctr_avg_raw=$(echo "$output" | cut -f4)
    max_dim=$(echo "$output" | cut -f5)

    # Parse stderr for node/face counts
    nodes=$(grep "^STL:" "$diag_file" | sed 's/STL: \([0-9]*\) nodes.*/\1/')
    faces=$(grep "^STEP:" "$diag_file" | sed 's/STEP: \([0-9]*\) faces/\1/')

... (truncated, 4429 chars)
```

```
/// Compute maximum distance between STL mesh vertices/centroids and STEP model surfaces.
///
/// Reads an STL file to extract vertex positions and triangle centroids, reads a
/// STEP file to extract surfaces, and computes the minimum distance from each
/// point to any STEP surface. Reports max distance for both vertices and centroids.
///
/// Units: distances are in the same units as the STEP file (typically mm).
use opencascade_sys::{
    b_rep, extrema, geom_api, gp, message, rw_stl, step_control, top_abs, top_exp, topo_ds,
};
use std::env;
use std::process;

fn main() {
    let args: Vec<String> = env::args().collect();
    if args.len() != 3 {
        eprintln!("Usage: {} <input.stl> <input.step>", args[0]);
        process::exit(1);
    }
    let stl_path = &args[1];
    let step_path = &args[2];

    // Read STL → Poly_Triangulation
    let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
    let tri_handle = rw_stl::read_file_charptr_progressrange_2(stl_path, &progress);
    let tri = tri_handle.get();
    let num_nodes = tri.nb_nodes();
    let num_triangles = tri.nb_triangles();
    eprintln!("STL: {} nodes, {} triangles", num_nodes, num_triangles);

    if num_nodes == 0 {
        eprintln!("Error: STL file has no vertices");
        process::exit(1);
    }

    // Read STEP
    let mut reader = step_control::Reader::new();
    reader.read_file_charptr(step_path);
    let progress2 = message::ProgressRange::new();
    reader.transfer_roots(&progress2);
    let step_shape = reader.one_shape();

    // Collect surfaces from all STEP faces
    let mut surfaces = Vec::new();
    let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
        &step_shape,
        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
    );
    while explorer.more() {
        let shape_ref = explorer.value();
        let face = topo_ds::face_shape(shape_ref);
        surfaces.push(b_rep::Tool::surface_face(face));
        explorer.next();
    }
    eprintln!("STEP: {} faces", surfaces.len());

    if surfaces.is_empty() {
        eprintln!("Error: STEP file has no faces");
        process::exit(1);
    }

    // Compute STL bounding box and max dimension
    let mut bb_min = [f64::MAX; 3];
    let mut bb_max = [f64::MIN; 3];
    for i in 1..=num_nodes {
        let pt = tri.node(i);
        let coords = [pt.x(), pt.y(), pt.z()];
        for d in 0..3 {
            bb_min[d] = bb_min[d].min(coords[d]);
            bb_max[d] = bb_max[d].max(coords[d]);
        }
    }
    let extents = [
        bb_max[0] - bb_min[0],
        bb_max[1] - bb_min[1],
        bb_max[2] - bb_min[2],
    ];
    let max_dimension = extents[0].max(extents[1]).max(extents[2]);
    eprintln!("Bounding box: {:.6} x {:.6} x {:.6}", extents[0], extents[1], extents[2]);
    eprintln!("Max dimension: {:.6}", max_dimension);

    let min_distance_to_surfaces = |pt: &gp::Pnt| -> f64 {
        let mut min_dist = f64::MAX;
        for surface in &surfaces {
            let projector = geom_api::ProjectPointOnSurf::new_pnt_handlegeomsurface_extalgo(
                pt,
                surface,
                extrema::ExtAlgo::Grad,
            );
            if projector.is_done() && projector.nb_points() > 0 {
                let d = projector.lower_distance();
                if d < min_dist {
                    min_dist = d;
                }
            }
        }
        min_dist
    };

    // Vertex distances
    let mut vtx_max_dist = 0.0_f64;
    let mut vtx_max_idx = 1_i32;
    let mut vtx_dist_sum = 0.0_f64;

    for i in 1..=num_nodes {
        let pt = tri.node(i);
        let min_dist = min_distance_to_surfaces(&pt);
        if min_dist < f64::MAX {
            vtx_dist_sum += min_dist;
            if min_dist > vtx_max_dist {
                vtx_max_dist = min_dist;
                vtx_max_idx = i;
            }
        }
    }

    // Centroid distances
    let mut ctr_max_dist = 0.0_f64;
    let mut _ctr_max_idx = 1_i32;
    let mut ctr_dist_sum = 0.
... (truncated, 5522 chars)
```

> 💭 I see two issues:
> 
> 1. **Missing MAX (medium) for onshape** — The medium group is not showing! Looking at the output, the onshape directory has medium files first (chamfered_cube, cone, cylinder), then fine (dome, pipe_elbow, rounded_cube, sphere), then l_bracket_simple_medium is mixed in between. Wait no, looking at the actual output order:
>    - chamfered_cube_10_c1_medium → medium
>    - cone_15x20_medium → medium
>    - cylinder_10x30_medium → medium
>    - dome_hemisphere_20_fine → fine
>    - l_bracket_simple_medium → medium
>    - pipe_elbow_10_fine → fine
>    - plate_with_hole_100x50_coarse → coarse
>    - rounded_cube_10_r2_fine → fine
>    - sphere_25_fine → fine
>    - stepped_block_coarse → coarse
> 
> The groups are interleaved: medium, medium, medium, fine, medium, fine, coarse, fine, fine, coarse. The associative array should handle this since it keys by group name, but I'm only seeing MAX output for fine and coarse — the medium group is missing entirely.
> 
> Looking at the output more carefully, there's no `** MAX (medium) **` line for onshape even though chamfered_cube, cone, cylinder, and l_bracket should all be in the medium group. The issue must be in how flush_groups is handling the group_order array.
> 
> Examining the flush_groups function... Looking at the chamfered_cube output, I see the formatted values are showing as zeros, but the raw values are actually very small numbers like 2.42e-07 for vertex max and 3.81e-04 for centroid max — these are being passed to the update_group_max function but getting truncated in the display. I'm checking how the raw values get passed through the update_group_max function and accumulated with awk comparisons across multiple files. The medium group should be building up correctly through all four iterations, but I'm noticing something odd about how flush_groups is being called.
> 
> Actually, I think I've found the real problem — when flush_groups runs at directory boundaries, it's flushing the medium group from the fusion directory. But the bigger issue is that typeset -A inside the function creates local variables instead of modifying the global ones, so the group tracking arrays aren't persisting the way they should be. The issue is that `group_order` gets reset properly since it's a regular array, but the associative arrays don't actually clear because `typeset -A` creates local shadows—so when the medium group is encountered in the onshape files, it's not recognized as new since `group_vtx_max[medium]` still holds the old fusion value. I can fix this by directly assigning empty values to the arrays instead of using `typeset -A`. Clearing the arrays...
> 
> This bug explains why onshape's medium group wasn't being tracked—fusion's medium data persisted in the global arrays since `flush_groups` was creating local shadowing variables instead of actually clearing them, so when onshape processed its medium files, the array already had a value and the group wasn't added to the order list again.

I can see the medium MAX is missing from onshape! The bug is in `flush_groups` — `typeset -A` inside a function creates **local** variables in zsh, so the global arrays never get cleared. The medium group from fusion persists, and when onshape's medium files are processed, `group_vtx_max[medium]` is non-empty so "medium" never gets added to `group_order`.

Let me fix that:

<details>
<summary>Reading lines 48-65 of <a href="../scripts/step_distance_table.sh#L48-L66">step_distance_table.sh</a></summary>

```
# Track per-group maximums
typeset -A group_vtx_max group_vtx_avg group_ctr_max group_ctr_avg
prev_dir=""
group_order=()

flush_groups() {
    if [[ ${#group_order[@]} -gt 0 ]]; then
        for g in "${group_order[@]}"; do
            local vm=$(fmt_vtx "${group_vtx_max[$g]}")
            local va=$(fmt_vtx "${group_vtx_avg[$g]}")
            local cm=$(fmt_ctr "${group_ctr_max[$g]}")
            local ca=$(fmt_ctr "${group_ctr_avg[$g]}")
            printf "$ROW_FMT" "  ** MAX ($g) **" "$vm" "$va" "$cm" "$ca" "" "" ""
        done
    fi
    typeset -A group_vtx_max group_vtx_avg group_ctr_max group_ctr_avg
    group_order=()
}
```
</details>

The commit is done (d46e3f2). Three fixes applied:

1. **Unit display**: Replaced `to_microns()` (incorrectly × 1e6) with `fmt_vtx()` (%.6f mm) and `fmt_ctr()` (%.3f mm)
